README.md162 lines
# ATLAS – Passive OSINT Intelligence Agent  (v3.0)

**ATLAS** is a modular, **standard-library-only** Python agent for ethical, passive open-source research. Give it an IP address, a domain or URL,
an organisation, a publicly known person or a username and it produces a sourced, confidence-rated report.

Version 3 is deliberately **leaner and wider**: the VPN manager, image gallery and location module from 2.x were removed (a full 2.1 snapshot
is kept separately), and the effort went into **more public sources**, a **much larger username search**, and **accuracy checks** that tell you
how far to trust each result.

```
    ___   ________  ___    ___   _____
   /   | /_  __/ / / / |  / / | / ___/
  / /| |  / / / / / /| | / /| | \__ \
 / ___ | / / / /_/ / | |/ / | |___/ /
/_/  |_|/_/  \____/  |___/  |_/____/
```

## What it can look up

| Target | Sources (all public, keyless) | Notes |
|--------|-------------------------------|-------|
| **IP address** | RDAP registries, **two** geolocation providers (cross-checked), DNS blocklists | IPv4/IPv6; private/reserved addresses get an explanatory report and **no** network traffic |
| **Domain / URL** | RDAP, DNS (own stdlib client), Certificate Transparency, **Internet Archive history**, **related domains**, **provider inference**, the site itself | robots.txt honoured |
| **Organisation** | **Wikidata** (official website, disambiguation), name-verified website candidates, CT-log domains certified to the name | a site is only "likely official" if its own title/name matches |
| **Public figure** | Wikipedia + **Wikidata** (candidate list, occupation, employer, official site), pages you supply with `--source` | refuses to merge ambiguous identities; confidence never HIGH automatically |
| **Username** | **79 mainstream platforms** (editable JSON list), control-checked | existence only; never identity |

> **Reality check.** No tool can "find everything on the internet": much of the web is behind logins, blocks automated access, or is only reachable
> through search engines that need keys/paid APIs. ATLAS reaches as far as public, permitted, keyless sources go and tells you what it could **not** check.

## Installation

Python **3.9+**. Nothing to install – ATLAS uses only the standard library.

```bash
cd atlas
python3 main.py --help        # Windows: py main.py --help
```

## Usage

```
python main.py                              # interactive shell
atlas> investigate 8.8.8.8
atlas> investigate example.com
atlas> investigate "Example Corp"
atlas> investigate "Jane Roe" --source https://example.org/official-bio
atlas> username jdoe_42                     # also: @jdoe_42, or a bare handle like jdoe_42
atlas> selftest                             # health-check the username platform rules
atlas> report | export html | reports list | reports delete <name|all> | reports purge <days>
atlas> offline on | offline off | help | exit
```

One-shot form (the original CLI still works):

```bash
python main.py investigate 8.8.8.8
python main.py -t example.com --no-report
python main.py investigate Tim Cook --type person          # quotes optional; options may come first or last
python main.py username jdoe_42 --stop-at-first
python main.py username jdoe_42 --platforms my_platforms.json
python main.py selftest
python main.py reports list
python main.py investigate Jane Roe --offline --no-save    # no network, nothing written
```

| Option | Meaning |
|--------|---------|
| `--type TYPE` | force `ip`, `domain`, `url`, `org`, `person`, `username` |
| `--source URL` | an official/public page you supply (repeatable, max 5): confirms an organisation's site or supplies a person's biography page |
| `--platforms FILE` | username scans: use your own platform list (JSON) instead of the built-in one |
| `--stop-at-first` | username scans: stop at the first confirmed match |
| `--html` | also write an HTML report (no scripts, escaped, strict CSP) |
| `--raw` | keep raw source payloads in exports (phone/email/address fields are always redacted) |
| `--no-report`, `--no-save` | don't print the full report / don't write any files |
| `--offline` | disable all network access |
| `--list-reports`, `--delete-reports`, `--purge-older-than DAYS` | retention controls |
| `-o DIR`, `-v` | output directory, debug logging |

## Username search

`username <name>` asks each platform whether a **public profile exists for that exact username**: one request per platform, robots.txt honoured,
no profile content stored, no logins, no spelling variants.

**Accuracy safeguards** (these matter more as the list grows):

1. **Per-platform format check** – a name that is invalid on a platform is never sent there.
2. **Soft-404 detection** – a 200 page whose title/heading says "not found" counts as *absent*.
3. **Two control checks** – when a name looks "found", two random names that cannot exist are tried on the same platform. If either also looks found the platform is reported **unreliable**, not as a match.
4. **Graded confidence** – HIGH (controls passed *and* the username appears in the page title/URL), MEDIUM (controls passed), LOW (controls inconclusive).
5. **Bounded retries** – transient 429/5xx/connection errors are retried once; a long `Retry-After` is respected, never evaded.
6. **Honest outcomes** – found · not found · unreliable · inconclusive · skipped (robots.txt / invalid format) · error; each listed by name.
7. **`selftest`** – health-checks every rule with a random-name negative control and, for 24 platforms, a known-good official account, and flags *stale* or *unreliable* rules so you can fix or remove them.

**Edit the list.** Platforms live in `atlas/data/platforms.json` (79 shipped). Each entry: `name`, `category`, `url` (with one `{}`), `kind` (`status` | `message` | `json`),
optional `profile_url`, `pattern`, `absent_text`, `exists_path`/`absent_path`/`absent_if_empty` (json), `absent_codes`, `known_good`. Use `--platforms FILE` to swap in your own.
ATLAS validates every entry (https only, one placeholder, no IP hosts) and **refuses dating, adult, messaging, health and finance categories** – those entries are skipped and reported.

**Same username ≠ same person.** Usernames are widely reused. Every match is marked identity *UNVERIFIED*; use results as leads, never as identification.
Platforms that block automated access (Instagram, X, TikTok, Facebook, LinkedIn, Reddit, YouTube, Threads, Pinterest) are **not queried**; ATLAS prints their profile URLs for you to open manually.

## Other accuracy checks

- **Geolocation cross-check** – ipinfo.io and ipwho.is (both HTTPS) are compared; agreement on country raises confidence, **disagreement is flagged as unreliable**. The cleartext ip-api.com is used only if both fail.
- **Registry vs geolocation** – the RDAP registry country is compared with the geolocation country and differences are explained (often normal for hosting/CDN networks).
- **Name verification** – a candidate organisation website must identify itself with the organisation's name (whole title segment for one-word names); look-alike or parked domains are labelled unrelated.
- **Wikidata disambiguation** – several people/organisations with the same name produce a candidate list, not a guess.
- **Network retries** – one bounded retry for transient failures across all sources.
- **Confidence everywhere** – every report has per-source reliability ratings, a confidence table and a limitations list.

## Reports

TXT, JSON and optional HTML with 10 sections: Investigation overview · Target classification · Main findings · Network information ·
Professional information · Sources consulted · Source reliability · Confidence assessments · Limitations · Privacy and legal considerations.
Every finding names its source; missing data is reported as missing, never invented. Files are written owner-only; coordinates/postcodes are scrubbed.

## Offline vs online

- **Need internet:** every lookup (RDAP, geolocation, CT logs, DNS records, blocklists, Wikipedia/Wikidata, Internet Archive, websites, username platforms, `--source` pages).
- **Work offline (`--offline`):** classification, private/reserved IP reports, report rendering/export and retention commands. Offline mode opens no sockets and makes no DNS queries.

## Project structure

```
atlas/
├── main.py                      # CLI + interactive shell
├── README.md  SECURITY_AND_LEGAL.md  CHANGELOG.md  requirements.txt (none needed)
├── atlas/
│   ├── agent.py  classifier.py  report.py  ui.py
│   ├── data/platforms.json      # editable username platform list
│   ├── modules/   ip  domain  org  person  username
│   ├── sources/   rdap  dns_lookup  geoip  cert_transparency  reputation  web_presence  web_pages
│   │              wikipedia (wikimedia)  wikidata  wayback  provider_hints  username_sources  page_metadata
│   └── utils/     network_safety  helpers  html_safe  source_reliability  retention
├── tests/                       # unittest, fully offline (183 tests)
├── examples/                    # sample reports
└── reports/                     # your exports (owner-only permissions)
```

## Tests

```bash
python -m unittest discover -s tests -v
```

Fully offline: a fake web layer feeds ATLAS's real request pipeline (with response sequences for retry tests), and a loopback server exercises the real pinned-connection and size-cap code.
Live behaviour of third-party sites cannot be tested offline – run `python main.py selftest` to check the username rules against the real sites.

## Adding sources

1. New module in `atlas/sources/` returning a dict with `queried_at`, `success`, `source`, `errors`.
2. Make every request through `atlas.utils.network_safety.safe_request` (never `urllib` directly); use `respect_robots=True` for HTML pages and `retries=1` for APIs.
3. Call it from the relevant `modules/*.py`, add `key_findings` with sources, and add offline tests with `tests/helpers.FakeWeb`.

Keep sources **passive**: nothing that attempts unauthorised access, credential testing, private-data collection or residential-location discovery.

## Legal & privacy

For legitimate research, defensive security, education and journalism using public sources. Public information is not automatically lawful to collect or use:
legality depends on purpose, the information, how it is processed and the circumstances. Information about identifiable people – including usernames – can be
personal data under UK GDPR / DPA 2018 even when public. Not legal advice. Read **`SECURITY_AND_LEGAL.md`**.
main.py226 lines
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
ATLAS – Passive OSINT Intelligence Agent
Entry point, interactive terminal interface and one-shot CLI.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import argparse
import logging
import os
import sys

sys.path.insert(0, os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__)))

from atlas import ui
from atlas.agent import AtlasAgent
from atlas.classifier import parse_forced_type
from atlas.modules.username_module import run_selftest
from atlas.utils import retention
from atlas.utils.helpers import clean_target


def setup_logging(verbose: bool = False) -> None:
    logging.basicConfig(level=logging.DEBUG if verbose else logging.WARNING,
                        format="%(asctime)s [%(levelname)s] %(name)s: %(message)s", datefmt="%H:%M:%S")


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Command handlers (shared by interactive mode and one-shot CLI)
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def do_investigate(agent, target, opts, show_report=True, html=False):
    force = parse_forced_type(opts.get("type"))
    report = agent.investigate(target, progress_callback=ui.progress_printer, force_type=force,
                               sources=opts.get("sources"), html=html,
                               stop_at_first=opts.get("stop_at_first", False),
                               platforms_path=opts.get("platforms"))
    if "classification" in report:
        ui.show_classification(report["classification"])
    if "error" in report:
        ui.print_error(f"{report['error']}. {report.get('suggestion', '')}")
        return 1
    ui.show_summary(report)
    if show_report:
        ui.print_report_text(agent.render_last_report())
    return 0


def do_selftest(platforms_path=None):
    res = run_selftest(progress_callback=ui.progress_printer, platforms_path=platforms_path)
    ui.show_selftest(res)
    return 0


def do_reports(agent, args):
    sub = (args[0].lower() if args else "list")
    if sub == "list":
        ui.show_reports(retention.list_reports(agent.output_dir)); return 0
    if sub == "delete" and len(args) >= 2:
        try:
            if args[1].lower() == "all":
                if not ui.ask_confirm(f"Permanently delete ALL stored reports in '{agent.output_dir}'?"):
                    ui.print_info("Cancelled."); return 1
                n = retention.delete_all(agent.output_dir)
            else:
                n = retention.delete_report(agent.output_dir, args[1])
            ui.print_info(f"Deleted {n} file(s)."); return 0
        except ValueError as e:
            ui.print_error(str(e)); return 1
    if sub == "purge" and len(args) >= 2:
        try:
            days = float(args[1])
        except ValueError:
            ui.print_error("Usage: reports purge <days>"); return 1
        ui.print_info(f"Deleted {retention.purge_older_than(agent.output_dir, days)} file(s) older than {days:g} days.")
        return 0
    ui.print_error("Usage: reports list | reports delete <name|all> | reports purge <days>")
    return 1


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Interactive mode
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def run_interactive(agent: AtlasAgent) -> None:
    ui.print_banner()
    ui.print_help()
    while True:
        try:
            line = input("atlas> ").strip()
        except (EOFError, KeyboardInterrupt):
            print("\nExiting ATLAS.")
            break
        if not line:
            continue
        toks = ui.split_command(line)
        cmd, rest = toks[0].lower(), toks[1:]
        try:
            if cmd in ("exit", "quit", "q"):
                print("Goodbye."); break
            elif cmd in ("help", "?"):
                ui.print_help()
            elif cmd == "report":
                ui.print_report_text(agent.render_last_report())
            elif cmd == "export":
                if not agent.last_report:
                    ui.print_error("No report to export.")
                else:
                    paths = agent.export_last(html=bool(rest and rest[0].lower() == "html"))
                    ui.print_info("Exported:\n  " + "\n  ".join(paths.values()))
            elif cmd == "reports":
                do_reports(agent, rest)
            elif cmd == "offline":
                on = bool(rest) and rest[0].lower() == "on"
                agent.set_offline(on)
                ui.print_info(f"Offline mode {'ON – no network access' if on else 'OFF'}.")
            elif cmd == "selftest":
                _t, o = ui.parse_target_args(rest)
                do_selftest(o.get("platforms"))
            elif cmd == "username":
                target, opts = ui.parse_target_args(rest)
                if not target:
                    ui.print_error("Usage: username <name> [--stop-at-first]")
                else:
                    opts["type"] = "username"
                    do_investigate(agent, clean_target(target), opts, show_report=False)
            else:
                body = rest if cmd == "investigate" else toks
                target, opts = ui.parse_target_args(body)
                if not target:
                    ui.print_error("Please provide a target after 'investigate'.")
                else:
                    do_investigate(agent, clean_target(target), opts, show_report=False)
        except ValueError as e:
            ui.print_error(str(e))
        except Exception as e:                      # keep the shell alive
            ui.print_error(str(e))
            logging.exception("Command failed")


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# One-shot CLI
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def build_parser() -> argparse.ArgumentParser:
    p = argparse.ArgumentParser(
        description="ATLAS – Passive OSINT Intelligence Agent",
        formatter_class=argparse.RawDescriptionHelpFormatter,
        epilog="""
Examples:
  python main.py                                   interactive shell
  python main.py investigate 8.8.8.8
  python main.py investigate example.com --html
  python main.py investigate Tim Cook --source https://example.org/about/tim
  python main.py username jdoe_42               which public platforms have this exact username
  python main.py @jdoe_42 --stop-at-first
  python main.py reports list
  python main.py -t 1.1.1.1 --no-report --offline
        """)
    p.add_argument("words", nargs="*", help="[investigate|username|selftest|reports] target or arguments")
    p.add_argument("-t", "--target", dest="target_opt", help="Target to investigate (alternative form)")
    p.add_argument("-o", "--output-dir", default="reports", help="Directory for exported reports (default: reports)")
    p.add_argument("--type", dest="force_type", metavar="TYPE", help="Force type: ip, domain, url, org, person")
    p.add_argument("--source", action="append", default=[], metavar="URL",
                   help="Official/public page you supply as a source (repeatable, max 5)")
    p.add_argument("--platforms", metavar="FILE", help="Username scans: use your own platform list (JSON) instead of the built-in one")
    p.add_argument("--stop-at-first", action="store_true", help="Username scans: stop at the first confirmed match")
    p.add_argument("--html", action="store_true", help="Also write an HTML report")
    p.add_argument("--raw", action="store_true", help="Keep raw source payloads in exports (contacts stay redacted)")
    p.add_argument("--no-report", action="store_true", help="Do not print the full text report")
    p.add_argument("--no-save", action="store_true", help="Do not write any report files")
    p.add_argument("--offline", action="store_true", help="No network access at all")
    p.add_argument("--list-reports", action="store_true", help="List stored reports and exit")
    p.add_argument("--delete-reports", action="store_true", help="Delete ALL stored reports (asks first) and exit")
    p.add_argument("--purge-older-than", type=float, metavar="DAYS", help="Delete stored reports older than DAYS and exit")
    p.add_argument("-v", "--verbose", action="store_true", help="Enable debug logging")
    return p


def main(argv=None) -> int:
    parser = build_parser()
    args = parser.parse_intermixed_args(argv)   # allows options before/between target words
    setup_logging(args.verbose)
    try:
        parse_forced_type(args.force_type)
    except ValueError as e:
        parser.error(str(e))

    agent = AtlasAgent(output_dir=args.output_dir, include_raw=args.raw, offline=args.offline, save=not args.no_save)

    if args.list_reports:
        ui.show_reports(retention.list_reports(args.output_dir)); return 0
    if args.purge_older_than is not None:
        return do_reports(agent, ["purge", str(args.purge_older_than)])
    if args.delete_reports:
        return do_reports(agent, ["delete", "all"])

    words = list(args.words)
    cmd = words[0].lower() if words else ""
    if cmd == "reports":
        return do_reports(agent, words[1:])
    if cmd == "selftest":
        return do_selftest(args.platforms)

    opts = {"type": args.force_type, "sources": args.source, "stop_at_first": args.stop_at_first,
            "platforms": args.platforms}
    if cmd == "username":
        words, opts["type"] = words[1:], "username"
    if cmd == "investigate":
        words = words[1:]
    target = clean_target(args.target_opt or " ".join(words))
    if target:
        ui.print_banner()
        try:
            return do_investigate(agent, target, opts, show_report=not args.no_report,
                                  html=args.html)
        except Exception as e:
            ui.print_error(str(e)); logging.exception("Investigation failed"); return 1
    run_interactive(agent)
    return 0


if __name__ == "__main__":
    sys.exit(main())
atlas/__init__.py8 lines
"""
ATLAS – Python OSINT Intelligence Agent
Passive, ethical, modular open-source intelligence research tool.
"""

__version__ = "3.0.0"
__author__ = "ATLAS Project"
__description__ = "Standalone passive OSINT intelligence research agent"
atlas/agent.py203 lines
"""
ATLAS Investigation Engine – core orchestration.

Pipeline: classify -> target module -> finalise (legal assessment, source reliability) -> (optional) export.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import logging
from typing import Any, Callable, Dict, List, Optional
from urllib.parse import urlparse

from . import __version__
from .classifier import ClassificationResult, TargetType, classify
from .modules.domain_module import investigate_domain
from .modules.ip_module import investigate_ip
from .modules.org_module import investigate_organisation
from .modules.person_module import investigate_person
from .modules.username_module import investigate_username
from .report import export_reports, render_text_report
from .sources import web_pages
from .utils.helpers import is_offline, set_offline, timestamp
from .utils.network_safety import ip_category, validate_url
from .utils.source_reliability import rate_source

logger = logging.getLogger("atlas.agent")

MAX_SOURCES = 5


class AtlasAgent:
    """Accepts a target, classifies it, runs the appropriate modules, produces a report."""

    def __init__(self, output_dir: str = "reports", include_raw: bool = False, offline: bool = False,
                 save: bool = True):
        self.output_dir = output_dir
        self.include_raw = include_raw      # raw RDAP payloads in exports (contacts always redacted)
        self.save = save                    # automatically export after each full investigation
        self.last_report: Optional[Dict[str, Any]] = None
        self.set_offline(offline)

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------ helpers
    @staticmethod
    def set_offline(value: bool) -> None:
        set_offline(value)

    def _context(self, sources: Optional[List[str]], use_wikipedia: bool = True) -> Dict[str, Any]:
        valid, rejected = [], []
        for url in (sources or [])[:MAX_SOURCES]:
            ok, why = validate_url(url)
            if ok:
                host = urlparse(url).hostname or ""
                try:                                    # IP literals can be judged without any lookup
                    if not ip_category(host)["is_global"]:
                        ok, why = False, "non-public IP address"
                except ValueError:
                    pass
            (valid if ok else rejected).append(url if ok else f"{url[:80]} ({why})")
        hosts = sorted({(urlparse(u).hostname or "").lower() for u in valid})
        return {"sources": valid, "official_domains": hosts, "rejected_sources": rejected,
                "use_wikipedia": use_wikipedia}

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------ full investigation
    def investigate(
        self,
        user_input: str,
        progress_callback: Optional[Callable[[str], None]] = None,
        export: bool = True,
        force_type: Optional[TargetType] = None,
        sources: Optional[List[str]] = None,
        html: bool = False,
        use_wikipedia: bool = True,
        stop_at_first: bool = False,
        platforms_path: Optional[str] = None,
    ) -> Dict[str, Any]:
        def progress(msg: str) -> None:
            if progress_callback:
                progress_callback(msg)
            logger.debug(msg)

        web_pages.clear_cache()
        c = classify(user_input, force=force_type)
        progress(f"Received target: {c.original}")
        progress(f"Classified as {c.target_type.name} (confidence {c.confidence:.2f}) – {c.notes}")
        ctx = self._context(sources, use_wikipedia)

        T = TargetType
        if c.target_type == T.IP:
            report = investigate_ip(c.normalised, progress_callback=progress)
        elif c.target_type in (T.DOMAIN, T.URL):
            report = investigate_domain(c.normalised, progress_callback=progress)
        elif c.target_type == T.ORGANISATION:
            report = investigate_organisation(c.normalised, progress_callback=progress)
        elif c.target_type == T.PERSON:
            report = investigate_person(c.normalised, progress_callback=progress,
                                        sources=ctx["sources"], use_wikipedia=use_wikipedia)
        elif c.target_type == T.USERNAME:
            report = investigate_username(c.normalised, progress_callback=progress, stop_at_first=stop_at_first,
                                          platforms_path=platforms_path)
        else:
            report = self._unknown_report(user_input, c)

        report.setdefault("key_findings", [])
        self._finalise(report, c, ctx)
        self.last_report = report

        if export and self.save and "error" not in report:
            self._export(report, progress, html=html)
        return report

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------ internals
    def _unknown_report(self, user_input: str, c: ClassificationResult) -> Dict[str, Any]:
        return {
            "target": user_input, "target_type": "UNKNOWN",
            "error": "Could not classify input with sufficient confidence",
            "suggestion": "Try a clearer IP address, domain name, organisation name, or publicly known person's name, "
                          "or force a type with --type.",
        }

    def _finalise(self, report: Dict[str, Any], c: ClassificationResult, ctx: Dict[str, Any]) -> None:
        report["classification"] = {
            "original_input": c.original, "normalised": c.normalised, "type": c.target_type.name,
            "confidence": c.confidence, "notes": c.notes,
        }
        report["atlas_version"] = __version__
        report["offline"] = is_offline()
        report["completed_at"] = report.get("completed_at") or timestamp()
        for r in ctx.get("rejected_sources", []):
            report.setdefault("limitations", []).append(f"Ignored unsafe/invalid --source URL: {r}")
        # source reliability table
        seen, rows = set(), []
        for s in report.get("sources_used", []):
            s = str(s)
            if s and s not in seen:
                seen.add(s)
                rating, why = rate_source(s)
                rows.append({"source": s, "rating": rating, "rationale": why})
        report["source_reliability"] = rows
        report["legal_assessment"] = self._legal_assessment(c.target_type)

    def _export(self, report: Dict[str, Any], progress, html: bool = False) -> None:
        try:
            paths = export_reports(report, self.output_dir, include_raw=self.include_raw, html=html)
            report["export"] = paths
            for p in paths.values():
                progress(f"Report exported → {p}")
        except Exception as e:
            logger.warning("Export failed: %s", e)
            report["export_error"] = str(e)

    def export_last(self, html: bool = False) -> Dict[str, str]:
        if not self.last_report:
            raise RuntimeError("No report to export.")
        paths = export_reports(self.last_report, self.output_dir, include_raw=self.include_raw, html=html)
        self.last_report["export"] = paths
        return paths

    def _legal_assessment(self, target_type: TargetType) -> Dict[str, Any]:
        base: Dict[str, Any] = {
            "disclaimer": (
                "This assessment is provided for transparency only and is not formal legal advice. Using publicly "
                "available information does not automatically make an investigation lawful: the position depends on "
                "jurisdiction, the purpose of processing, the data involved, how it is collected and used, and the "
                "circumstances. Seek qualified legal advice for your use case."),
            "primary_frameworks_considered": ["UK GDPR", "Data Protection Act 2018",
                                              "Computer Misuse Act 1990", "PECR"],
            "activity_classification": {
                "public_rdap_dns_ct_lookups": {"classification": "Generally permitted public research",
                    "notes": "RDAP, DNS and Certificate Transparency are designed as public data sources."},
                "passive_http_observation": {"classification": "Generally permitted public research",
                    "notes": "Requests to public pages honour robots.txt; a single request is visible in the site's logs."},
                "geolocation_of_public_ip": {"classification": "Generally permitted public research (with caveats)",
                    "notes": "Approximate network location only. An IP can be personal data in context."},
                "blocklist_reputation_checks": {"classification": "Generally permitted public research",
                    "notes": "Standard passive DNS-based blocklist queries; indicators only."},
                "automated_scraping_of_registries_or_social": {"classification": "Potentially restricted or regulated",
                    "notes": "May breach terms or data-protection rules. ATLAS avoids this."},
                "unauthorised_access_or_credential_testing": {"classification": "Not permitted by the system",
                    "notes": "Prohibited under the Computer Misuse Act 1990 and equivalent laws."},
                "collection_of_private_personal_data": {"classification": "Not permitted by the system",
                    "notes": "ATLAS does not seek private addresses, phones, accounts, credentials or residential locations."},
                "facial_recognition_or_reverse_image_search": {"classification": "Not permitted by the system",
                    "notes": "Not implemented; ATLAS never identifies people from images."},
            },
        }
        if target_type == TargetType.USERNAME:
            base["activity_classification"]["username_account_enumeration"] = {
                "classification": "Requires careful assessment; purpose and proportionality matter",
                "notes": "Checks only whether a public profile page exists for an exact username on platforms that permit "
                         "automated access (robots.txt honoured). A username can be personal data; do not use results to "
                         "identify, locate, monitor or harass an individual."}
        if target_type == TargetType.PERSON:
            base["activity_classification"]["person_focused_research"] = {
                "classification": "Requires careful assessment; a lawful basis is needed",
                "notes": "Information about an identifiable living individual is personal data even when public; "
                         "UK GDPR principles (purpose limitation, minimisation, accuracy, storage limitation) apply."}
        return base

    def render_last_report(self) -> str:
        if not self.last_report:
            return "No report available."
        return render_text_report(self.last_report)
atlas/classifier.py178 lines
"""
Input classification for ATLAS.
Determines whether the target is an IP, domain, URL, organisation, or person.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import re
from dataclasses import dataclass
from enum import Enum, auto
from typing import Optional
from urllib.parse import urlparse

from .sources.username_sources import USERNAME_RE, validate_username
from .utils.helpers import (
    clean_target,
    is_valid_domain,
    is_valid_ipv4,
    is_valid_ipv6,
    is_valid_url,
)


class TargetType(Enum):
    IP = auto()
    DOMAIN = auto()
    URL = auto()
    ORGANISATION = auto()
    PERSON = auto()
    USERNAME = auto()
    UNKNOWN = auto()


@dataclass
class ClassificationResult:
    original: str
    target_type: TargetType
    normalised: str
    confidence: float
    notes: str = ""


# Matched as whole words (so "Lincoln" no longer matches "inc").
ORG_INDICATORS = frozenset({
    "inc", "ltd", "llc", "llp", "plc", "gmbh", "corp", "corporation", "co",
    "company", "group", "holdings", "technologies", "systems", "solutions",
    "services", "foundation", "university", "college", "institute", "agency",
    "ministry", "bank", "council", "association", "society", "partners",
    "united", "kingdom", "states", "republic", "department", "authority",
})

# Words that may appear lowercase inside a proper name / org name.
_CONNECTORS = frozenset({"of", "the", "and", "for", "de", "van", "von", "la", "le", "al", "bin", "&"})


def parse_forced_type(value: Optional[str]) -> Optional[TargetType]:
    """Map a user-supplied type name (e.g. from --type) to a TargetType."""
    if not value:
        return None
    mapping = {
        "ip": TargetType.IP, "domain": TargetType.DOMAIN, "url": TargetType.URL,
        "org": TargetType.ORGANISATION, "organisation": TargetType.ORGANISATION,
        "organization": TargetType.ORGANISATION, "person": TargetType.PERSON,
        "username": TargetType.USERNAME, "user": TargetType.USERNAME, "handle": TargetType.USERNAME,
    }
    try:
        return mapping[value.strip().lower()]
    except KeyError:
        raise ValueError(f"Unknown target type {value!r}; choose from: {', '.join(sorted(mapping))}")


def _redact_url(url: str) -> str:
    """Drop any user:password@ section so credentials never reach a report."""
    parsed = urlparse(url)
    if parsed.username or parsed.password:
        return url.replace(parsed.netloc, parsed.netloc.rsplit("@", 1)[-1], 1)
    return url


def classify(user_input: str, force: Optional[TargetType] = None) -> ClassificationResult:
    """
    Automatically classify the user input (or honour an explicit override).
    Returns a ClassificationResult with type and normalised form.
    """
    raw = clean_target(user_input)
    if not raw:
        return ClassificationResult(raw, TargetType.UNKNOWN, "", 0.0, "Empty input")

    if force is not None:
        return _forced(raw, force)

    # 1. URL – use .hostname so credentials, ports and IPv6 brackets are handled.
    if is_valid_url(raw):
        host = (urlparse(raw).hostname or "").lower()
        if is_valid_ipv4(host) or is_valid_ipv6(host):
            return ClassificationResult(
                _redact_url(raw), TargetType.IP, host, 0.95,
                "URL whose host is an IP address; investigating the IP",
            )
        return ClassificationResult(
            _redact_url(raw), TargetType.URL, host, 0.95,
            "Treated as URL; investigation focuses on host/domain",
        )

    # 1b. Explicit handle: @name
    if raw.startswith("@") and validate_username(raw[1:])[0]:
        return ClassificationResult(raw, TargetType.USERNAME, raw[1:], 0.9, "Handle (leading @) – username presence check")

    # 2. IPv4 / IPv6
    if is_valid_ipv4(raw):
        return ClassificationResult(raw, TargetType.IP, raw, 0.99, "IPv4 address")
    if is_valid_ipv6(raw):
        return ClassificationResult(raw, TargetType.IP, raw.strip("[]"), 0.99, "IPv6 address")

    # 3. Domain (strip scheme-less prefixes / paths)
    candidate = raw.lower()
    for prefix in ("www.",):
        if candidate.startswith(prefix):
            candidate = candidate[len(prefix):]
    candidate = candidate.split("/")[0].split("?")[0].rstrip(".")
    if is_valid_domain(candidate):
        return ClassificationResult(
            raw, TargetType.DOMAIN, candidate, 0.90, "Looks like a domain name"
        )

    # 3b. Single token that looks like a platform handle (has a digit or underscore): john_smith99
    if " " not in raw and USERNAME_RE.match(raw) and re.search(r"[0-9_]", raw):
        return ClassificationResult(
            raw, TargetType.USERNAME, raw, 0.6,
            "Single token with digits/underscore – treated as a username (low confidence). Use --type org|person to override.")

    # 4. Person vs organisation heuristic (intentionally conservative)
    words = re.findall(r"[^\W_]+(?:['’.-][^\W_]+)*|&", raw, re.UNICODE)
    lowered = {w.lower() for w in words}
    significant = [w for w in words if w.lower() not in _CONNECTORS]
    all_capitalised = bool(significant) and all(w[0].isupper() or w.isupper() for w in significant)

    if len(significant) >= 2 and all_capitalised:
        if lowered & ORG_INDICATORS:
            return ClassificationResult(
                raw, TargetType.ORGANISATION, raw, 0.70, "Contains organisation indicators"
            )
        return ClassificationResult(
            raw, TargetType.PERSON, raw, 0.55,
            "Multi-word capitalised name – treated as public figure / person (low confidence). "
            "Use --type org to override.",
        )

    return ClassificationResult(
        raw, TargetType.ORGANISATION, raw, 0.50,
        "Treated as organisation / entity name (heuristic). Use --type to override.",
    )


def _forced(raw: str, force: TargetType) -> ClassificationResult:
    """Normalise the input for an explicitly requested type."""
    note = "Type set explicitly by user"
    if force in (TargetType.DOMAIN, TargetType.URL):
        host = raw
        if is_valid_url(raw):
            host = urlparse(raw).hostname or raw
        host = host.lower().split("/")[0].split("?")[0].rstrip(".")
        if host.startswith("www."):
            host = host[4:]
        return ClassificationResult(raw, force, host, 1.0, note)
    if force == TargetType.USERNAME:
        name = raw.lstrip("@")
        ok, why = validate_username(name)
        if not ok:
            return ClassificationResult(raw, TargetType.UNKNOWN, raw, 0.0, f"Invalid username: {why}")
        return ClassificationResult(raw, force, name, 1.0, note)
    if force == TargetType.IP:
        host = urlparse(raw).hostname if is_valid_url(raw) else raw.strip("[]")
        if not (is_valid_ipv4(host or "") or is_valid_ipv6(host or "")):
            return ClassificationResult(raw, TargetType.UNKNOWN, raw, 0.0, f"{raw!r} is not a valid IP address")
        return ClassificationResult(raw, force, host, 1.0, note)
    return ClassificationResult(raw, force, raw, 1.0, note)
